\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathbb{N}}\penalty\ \sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\lambda,\eta,s}}1_{I}(n)g_{I,k,\mathrm{psd}}(n),

\displaystyle c_{\lambda,\eta,k,i}(n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathbb{N}}\penalty\ \sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\lambda,\eta,s}}1_{I}(n)c_{I,k,i},

\displaystyle\alpha_{\lambda,\eta,k,i}(n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathbb{N}}\penalty\ \sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\lambda,\eta,s}}1_{I}(n)\alpha_{I,k,i},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\lambda,\eta,k}^{\star}(n)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathbb{N}}\penalty\ \sum_{I\in\mathcal{I}_{\lambda,\eta,s}}1_{I}(n)\mathcal{P}_{I,k}^{\star}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}^{\star}}c_{\lambda,\eta,k,i}\,e(n\alpha_{\lambda,\eta,k,i}).

From (
8.15
) we conclude

\|f_{\lambda,\eta,k,\mathrm{str}}-\mathcal{P}_{\lambda,\eta,k}^{\star}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{k},

(8.19)

from (
8.17
) we get

\left\langle f_{\lambda,\eta,k,\mathrm{str}},f_{\lambda,\eta,k,\mathrm{psd}}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{N}}=0,\hskip 20.00003pt\forall k\in\mathbb{N},

(8.20)

and from
(
8.18
) we see that

\left\langle f_{\lambda,\eta,k,\mathrm{str}},f_{\lambda,\eta,\ell,\mathrm{psd}}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{N}}\leqslant 2\varepsilon_{k},\hskip 20.00003pt\forall k<\ell\in\mathbb{N}.

(8.21)

Furthermore, from (
8.12
) and (
8.16
) it follows that

\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\ell,\mathrm{psd}}\|_{u^{2}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}\leqslant\varepsilon_{\ell},\hskip 10.00002pt\text{whenever}\penalty\ \mathbf{H}_{\lambda,\eta}\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}

(8.22)

which implies that

\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\ell,\mathrm{psd}}\|_{u^{2}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H}_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}})}\leqslant\varepsilon_{\ell},\hskip 10.00002pt\text{whenever}\penalty\ (\lambda,\eta)\prec(\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}).

This gives

\left\langle f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},k,\mathrm{str}},f_{\lambda,\eta,\ell,\mathrm{psd}}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{N}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{k}+\varepsilon_{\ell}d_{k}^{\star}\leqslant 2\varepsilon_{k},\hskip 10.00002pt\text{whenever}\penalty\ (\lambda,\eta)\prec(\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}).

(8.23)

In light of (
8.19
) and (
8.20
), we can invoke
7.12
to find
f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}}\colon\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]
and
f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{unf}}\colon\mathbb{N}\to[-1,1]
such that

f=f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}}+f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{unf}}
,
\left\langle f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}},f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{unf}}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{N}}=0
,
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}}-f_{\lambda,\eta,k,\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=0
, and
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{unf}}-f_{\lambda,\eta,k,\mathrm{psd}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=0
.
By (
8.23
), we have

\left\langle f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}},f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{unf}}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{N}}=0,\hskip 10.00002pt\text{whenever}\penalty\ (\lambda,\eta)\prec(\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}).

This implies

\displaystyle\|f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}},f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}},f\right\rangle_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}},f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}}\right\rangle_{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}\cdot\|f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}},

and hence

(\lambda,\eta)\prec(\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})\implies\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}\geqslant\|f_{\lambda^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}.

According to
8.8

there exists
\lambda\in(0,1)
such that

\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=\|f_{\lambda,\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}
for all
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
. In particular, we have for all
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in[0,1]
that

\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{str}}-f_{\lambda,\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=\|f_{\lambda,\eta,\mathrm{unf}}-f_{\lambda,\eta^{\prime},\mathrm{unf}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=0.

Now define
\mathbf{H}^{-}=\mathbf{H}_{\lambda,0}
and
\mathbf{H}^{+}=\mathbf{H}_{\lambda,1}
, as well as

f_{\mathrm{str}}=f_{\lambda,0,\mathrm{str}}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{and}\hskip 20.00003ptf_{\mathrm{unf}}=f_{\lambda,0,\mathrm{unf}}.

Moreover, define

\displaystyle c_{k,i}(n)

\displaystyle=c_{\lambda,1,k,i}(n),

\displaystyle\alpha_{k,i}(n)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\lambda,1,k,i}(n),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}^{\star}_{\lambda,1,k}=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}^{\star}}c_{k,i}\,e(n\alpha_{k,i}),

and note that since
\|\mathcal{P}_{k}-f_{\lambda,1,k,\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}\leqslant\varepsilon_{k}
,
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|f_{\lambda,1,\mathrm{str}}-f_{\lambda,1,k,\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=0
, and
\|f_{\mathrm{str}}-f_{\lambda,1,\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=0
, we have

\lim_{k\to\infty}\|f_{\mathrm{str}}-\mathcal{P}_{k}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=0.

(8.24)

By construction, for all but finitely many
s
we have that

c_{k,i}(n)\penalty\ \text{and}\penalty\ \alpha_{k,i}(n)\penalty\ \text{are constant on subintervals longer than $\geqslant H_{s}^{+}/2$ in $\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}$.}

(8.25)

Also, using
8.4
, after replacing
\mathbf{H}^{-}
and
\mathbf{H}^{+}
with a narrower pair of sequences if needed, we get that for all
C\in\mathbb{N}
and all
(w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}^{\star}})\in[-C,C]^{d_{k}^{\star}}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d_{k}^{\star}}
,
if
\mathbf{H}^{-}\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{H}^{+}
and

\displaystyle V_{s,w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}^{\star}},C}=\Bigg\{n\in[N_{s}

\displaystyle-H_{s}+1]:\Bigg|\frac{1}{C^{2}H_{s}}\sum_{h=1}^{C^{2}H_{s}}e(h(w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}^{\star}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}^{\star}}(n)))\,

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor}\sum_{h=1}^{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor}e(h(w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}^{\star}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}^{\star}}(n)))\Bigg|<\frac{1}{C^{2}}\Bigg\}

then

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|V_{s,w_{1},\ldots,w_{d},C}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=1.

(8.26)

We claim that if we consider

\displaystyle W_{s,k,C}=\Big\{n\in[N_{s}-H_{s}+1]:\text{for all}\penalty\ (w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}^{\star}})\in[-C,C]^{d_{k}^{\star}}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d_{k}^{\star}},\penalty\ \text{either}

\displaystyle\|w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}^{\star}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}^{\star}}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{CH_{s}}\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \|w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}^{\star}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}^{\star}}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\tfrac{C}{H_{s}}\Big\},

then

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|W_{s,k,C}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=1.

(8.27)

The sets
W_{s,k,C}
satisfy
W_{s,k,1}\supseteq W_{s,k,2}\supseteq\ldots
, so it suffices to prove the claim for large
C
.
We will show that

W_{s,k,C}\supseteq\bigcap_{(w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}^{\star}})\in[-C,C]^{d_{k}^{\star}}\cap\mathbb{Z}}V_{s,w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}^{\star}},C}

for
C\geqslant 4
and
s
sufficiently large (depending on
C
), from which the claim the follows by applying (
8.26
).
Fix
n\in V_{s,w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}^{\star}},C}
, and let
\beta=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}^{\star}}w_{i}\alpha_{k,i}
.
We want to show (assuming
s
is sufficiently large) that either
\|\beta\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant\frac{1}{CH_{s}}
or
\|\beta\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\frac{C}{H_{s}}
.
Suppose for contradiction that
\frac{1}{CH_{s}}<\|\beta\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\frac{C}{H_{s}}
.
Then

\left|\frac{1}{C^{2}H_{s}}\sum_{h=1}^{C^{2}H_{s}}e(h\beta)\right|=\frac{|e(C^{2}H_{s}\beta)-1|}{C^{2}H_{s}|e(\beta)-1|}\leqslant\frac{2}{C^{2}H_{s}\cdot 4\|\beta\|_{\mathbb{T}}}<\frac{1}{2C},

while

\left|\frac{1}{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor}\sum_{h=1}^{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor}e(h\beta)\right|=\frac{|e(\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor\beta)-1|}{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor|e(\beta)-1|}\geqslant\frac{4\|\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor\beta\|_{\mathbb{T}}}{(H_{s}/C^{2})\cdot 2\pi\|\beta\|_{\mathbb{T}}}\\
>\frac{2\left(\frac{H_{s}}{C^{2}}-1\right)\frac{1}{CH_{s}}}{\pi\frac{H_{s}}{C^{2}}\frac{C}{H_{s}}}=\frac{2}{\pi}\frac{H_{s}-C}{C^{2}H_{s}}=\frac{2}{\pi}\cdot\frac{1}{C^{2}}-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1).

Therefore,

\left|\frac{1}{C^{2}H_{s}}\sum_{h=1}^{C^{2}H_{s}}e(h\beta)-\frac{1}{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor}\sum_{h=1}^{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor}e(h\beta)\right|\geqslant\frac{1}{2C}-\frac{2}{\pi}\frac{1}{C^{2}}-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)

For
C\geqslant 4
, this contradicts the assumption
n\in V_{s,w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}^{\star}},C}
.
This proves the claim.

Now let
\varepsilon>0
and
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\mathbf{H}^{-}\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{H}^{+}
be arbitrary.
Let
k
be sufficiently large so that
\|f_{\mathrm{str}}-f_{\lambda,1,k,\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}\leqslant\varepsilon/2
and
\varepsilon_{k}\leqslant\varepsilon/2
, and define
d=d_{k}^{\star}
and
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{k}
.
From (
8.27
), (
8.27
), and (
8.27
) it follows that with this choice of
d
and
\mathcal{P}
part
(ii)
of
8.6
is satisfied.
Since
\mathbf{H}_{\lambda,0}=\mathbf{H}^{-}\prec\mathbf{H}
, it follows from (
8.22
) that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\|f_{\lambda,0,k,\mathrm{psd}}\|_{u^{2}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}=0.

Combined with
\lim_{k\to\infty}\|f_{\lambda,0,\mathrm{unf}}-f_{\lambda,0,k,\mathrm{unf}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=0
, and since
f_{\mathrm{unf}}=f_{\lambda,0,\mathrm{unf}}
, we conclude that

\|f_{\mathrm{unf}}\|_{u^{2}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}=0.

It thus follows from (
8.10
) that
\|f_{\mathrm{unf}}\|_{U^{2}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}=0
and so part
(iii)
of
8.6
holds too.
∎

We showed in
8.5
(i)
that if a function
f:\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]
admits correlations along a sequence
\mathbf{N}
and
\mathbf{H}
is a sufficiently slowly growing scale, then the
U^{1}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
seminorm of
f
agrees with the Host–Kra
U^{1}
seminorm of
f
along
\mathbf{N}
.
The next lemma, which is needed for technical arguments in
Section

13
below, establishes a similar result for the
U^{2}
seminorms.

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
f:\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]
and suppose
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of natural numbers such that
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}=\infty
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
.
If
f
admits correlations along
\mathbf{N}
, then there exists a sequence of natural numbers
\mathbf{H}_{HK,U^{2}}=(H_{HK,U^{2},s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}_{HK,U^{2}}\prec\mathbf{N}
such that for all sequences
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}\preceq\mathbf{H}_{HK,U^{2}}
, the pair
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
f
with the property that the corresponding decomposition
f=f_{\mathrm{str}}+f_{\mathrm{unf}}
given by
8.6
for the
U^{2}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
seminorm satisfies

\|f_{\mathrm{str}}-f_{1}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=\|f_{\mathrm{unf}}-f_{2}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}=0,

where
f=f_{1}+f_{2}
is the decomposition given by
7.15
for the
U^{2}(\mathbf{N})
Host–Kra seminorm.

Proof.

By
7.15
, there exist locally invariant functions
c_{k,1},\ldots,c_{k,d_{k}}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{C}
with
\|c_{k,i}\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1
and locally invariant functions
\alpha_{k,1},\ldots,\alpha_{k,d_{k}}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{T}
such that if
\mathcal{P}_{k}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{d_{k}}c_{k,i}(n)e(n\alpha_{k,i}(n))
, then

\|f_{1}-\mathcal{P}_{k}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}<\frac{1}{k}.

Then by
8.5
, for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
\mathbf{H}_{HK,k}=(H_{HK,k,s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}_{HK,k}\prec\mathbf{N}
such that if
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
1\prec\mathbf{H}\preceq\mathbf{H}_{HK,k}
, then the functions
c_{k,i}
and
\alpha_{k,i}
can be chosen to be
U^{1}
-structured in the sense of
8.1
for
i\in[d_{k}]
.
We may pick a sequence
K_{s}\to\infty
such that
\lim_{s\to\infty}\min_{k\in[K_{s}]}H_{HK,k,s}=\infty
and define
H_{HK,U^{2},s}=\min_{k\in[K_{s}]}H_{HK,k,s}
.
Then
1\prec\mathbf{H}_{HK,U^{2}}
and
\mathbf{H}_{HK,U^{2}}\preceq\mathbf{H}_{HK,k}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let us check that
\mathbf{H}_{HK,U^{2}}
has the desired properties.
Suppose
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
1\prec\mathbf{H}\preceq\mathbf{H}_{HK,U^{2}}
.
We want to show that the decomposition
f=f_{1}+f_{2}
satisfies the nonnegativity, structure, and uniformity properties in
8.6
.
Nonnegativity (property
(i)
) and orthogonality (property
(iii)
) follow from nonnegativity and orthogonality in
7.15
.
Given
\varepsilon>0
, we may pick
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\frac{1}{k}\leqslant\varepsilon
.
Then

\|f_{1}-\mathcal{P}_{k}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}<\frac{1}{k}\leqslant\varepsilon,

and the sets

\displaystyle\Gamma_{s,k}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\left\{n\in[N_{s}-H_{s}+1]:\alpha_{k,i}(n+m_{1})=\alpha_{k,i}(n+m_{2})\penalty\ \forall i\in[d_{k}],\penalty\ \forall m_{1},m_{2}\in[H_{s}]\right\}

and

\displaystyle\Gamma_{s,k}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\left\{n\in[N_{s}-H_{s}+1]:c_{k,i}(n+m_{1})=c_{k,i}(n+m_{2})\penalty\ \forall i\in[d_{k}],\penalty\ \forall m_{1},m_{2}\in[H_{s}]\right\}

satisfy

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|\Gamma_{s,k}^{(1)}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|\Gamma_{s,k}^{(2)}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=1

since
\mathbf{H}\preceq\mathbf{H}_{HK,k}
.
Moreover, if
C\in\mathbb{N}
and
(w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}})\in[-C,C]^{d_{k}}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d_{k}}
, then the function
g(n)=e(w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}}(n))
is
U^{1}
structured, so

\displaystyle V_{s,k,C}=\Bigg\{n\in[N_{s}

\displaystyle-H_{s}+1]:\Bigg|\frac{1}{C^{2}H_{s}}\sum_{h=1}^{C^{2}H_{s}}e(h(w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}}(n)))\,

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor}\sum_{h=1}^{\lfloor H_{s}/C^{2}\rfloor}e(h(w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}}(n)))\Bigg|<\frac{1}{C^{2}}\Bigg\}

satisfies

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|V_{s,k,C}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=1.

Then arguing as in the proof of
8.7
, we have that the set

\displaystyle W_{s,k,C}=\Big\{n\in[N_{s}-H_{s}+1]:\text{for all}\penalty\ (w_{1},\ldots,w_{d_{k}})\in[-C,C]^{d_{k}}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d_{k}},\penalty\ \text{either}

\displaystyle\|w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{CH_{s}}\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \|w_{1}\alpha_{k,1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d_{k}}\alpha_{k,d_{k}}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\tfrac{C}{H_{s}}\Big\},

also satisfies

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|W_{s,k,C}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=1.

Thus, the decomposition
f=f_{1}+f_{2}
also satisfies
(ii)
from
8.6
.
∎

9.  Sumsets and
\delta
-popular sumsets in the integers at intermediate scales

In this section, we apply the structure theorems for the intermediate scale seminorms from
Section

8
to sumsets in
\mathbb{N}
.
Given
Q\in\mathbb{N}
and two sets
E,F\subseteq\{0,1,\ldots,Q-1\}
, we write
E+_{\bmod{Q}}F
to denote the sumset reduced mod
Q
:

\displaystyle E+_{\bmod{Q}}F

\displaystyle=\{x+y\bmod{Q}:x\in E,y\in F\}

\displaystyle=\{x+y:x\in E,y\in F,x+y<Q\}\cup\{x+y-Q:x\in E,y\in F,x+y\geqslant Q\}.

The main result of this section is the following.

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
A\subseteq\mathbb{N}
such that
d(A)>0
, and let
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be sequences in
\mathbb{N}
with

1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
.
If
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
A
, then there exists a sequence
\varepsilon_{s}\to 0
such that for every
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, there exists
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s})H_{s}
such that
Q_{n}
is highly divisible in the sense that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\min\{m\in\mathbb{N}:m\nmid Q_{n}\}=\infty,

and for any set
\tilde{B}_{s}\subseteq[H_{s}]
, for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, there exist sets
A_{n}\subseteq(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
and
B_{n}\subseteq\tilde{B}_{s}
such that:

(I)

|(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash A_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
,

(II)

|\tilde{B}_{s}\backslash B_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
,

(III)

|(A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n})\backslash((A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\})|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
.

Moreover, if
\tilde{B}_{s}=B\cap[H_{s}]
for each
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
B
meets every residue class in
\mathbb{N}
, then there exists a sequence
D_{s}\to\infty
such that each
B_{n}
may be chosen to satisfy

(IV)

B_{n}
meets every residue class mod
m\leqslant D_{s}
.

9.1.  A new proof of a corollary of Kneser’s theorem

One immediate application of
9.1
is a new proof of the following corollary of Kneser’s theorem (see
1.3
).

Corollary 9.2
.

Let
A,B\subseteq\mathbb{N}
, and suppose
d(A),d(B)>0
(in particular, the density exists).
Suppose moreover that
B
meets every residue class in
\mathbb{N}
.
Then

\underline{d}(A+B)\geqslant\min\{1,d(A)+d(B)\}.

Proof.

Let
\alpha=d(A)
and
\beta=d(B)
.
Let
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
such that
\underline{d}(A+B)=d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
.
Let
\mathbf{H}_{G}=(H_{G,s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be a Gowers scale for
A
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}_{G}\prec\mathbf{N}
as given by
8.5
(ii)
.
Then by
8.7
, let
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be a
U^{2}
good scale for
A
with
\mathbf{H}_{G}\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
.

By
9.1
, let
\varepsilon_{s}\to 0
,
D_{s}\to\infty
, and
G_{s}\subseteq\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
with
|G_{s}|=(1-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))N_{s}
such that if
n\in G_{s}
, then there exists
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s})H_{s}
and sets
A_{n}\subseteq(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
and
B_{n}\subseteq B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
such that:

•

|(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash A_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
,

•

|B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash B_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
,

•

B_{n}
meets every residue class mod
m
for
m\leqslant D_{s}
, and

•

|(A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n})\backslash((A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\})|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
.

Since
\mathbf{H}\succ\mathbf{H}_{G}
, we may additionally assume that
|(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}|=(\alpha+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n}
for
n\in G_{s}
.
Therefore, the first two bullet points yield
|A_{n}|=(\alpha+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n}
and
|B_{n}|=(\beta+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n}
.

We now proceed by splitting the proof into cases.

Case 1.
\alpha+\beta\geqslant 1
.

Let
n\in G_{s}
.
By
4.4
, either
A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}=\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
or

|(A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}|\geqslant|A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}|-\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}\\
\geqslant|A_{n}|+|B_{n}|-D_{s}^{-1}Q_{n}-\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}=\left(\alpha+\beta-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)\right)Q_{n}\geqslant(1-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n}.

Thus, for every
n\in G_{s}
,

|(A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}|=(1-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n}.

Averaging over
n\leqslant N_{s}
,

\underline{d}(A+B)=d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{|(A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}|}{Q_{n}}=1.

Case 2.
\alpha+\beta<1
.

In this case, for all large enough
s\in\mathbb{N}
, if
n\in G_{s}
, then
|A_{n}|+|B_{n}|<Q_{n}
.
Therefore, by
4.4
,

\displaystyle|(A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}|

\displaystyle\geqslant|A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}|-\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}

\displaystyle\geqslant|A_{n}|+|B_{n}|-D_{s}^{-1}Q_{n}-\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}

\displaystyle=\left(\alpha+\beta-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)\right)Q_{n}.

Averaging over
n\leqslant N_{s}
,

\underline{d}(A+B)=d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{|(A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}|}{Q_{n}}\geqslant\alpha+\beta.

∎

9.2.  Convolution at intermediate scales

Given sequences
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
and bounded functions
f,g\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{C}
,
we define the convolution of
f
with
g
at scale
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
as

f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n)=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{N}}1_{(N_{t-1},N_{t}]}(n)\Bigg(\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}f(n-m)g(m)\Bigg),

with the convention that
N_{0}=0
.
This form of convolution is non-commutative. Its purpose is to capture the additive interaction between the values of
f(n)
for
n\in[N_{s}]
and those of
g(h)
for
h\in[H_{s}]
.

The main result of this subsection is the following:

Theorem 9.3
.

Let
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]
, and suppose
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
are sequences in
\mathbb{N}
with

1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
. Suppose
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
f
, and let
f=f_{\mathrm{str}}+f_{\mathrm{unf}}
be the associated splitting. Then

\lim_{s\to\infty}\sup_{g:\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]}\|f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g\,-\,f_{\mathrm{str}}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g\|_{2,[N_{s}]}=0.

The main ingredient in the proof of
9.3
is the following proposition.

Proposition 9.4
.

For all sequences
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\mathbf{H}_{1}=(H_{1,s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
, and
\mathbf{H}_{2}=(H_{2,s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with

1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2}\prec\mathbf{N}

and any bounded
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{C}
we have that
\|f\|_{U^{2}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}=0
implies

\begin{split}\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\sup_{u_{1},u_{2},g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{H_{1,s}}\sum_{h_{1}=-H_{1,s}}^{H_{1,s}}\frac{1}{H_{2,s}}\sum_{h_{2}=-H_{2,s}}^{H_{2,s}}u_{1}(&h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})g_{1}(n)g_{2}(n+h_{1})\\
&\cdot g_{3}(n+h_{2})\overline{f(n+h_{1}+h_{2})}\Bigg|=0,\end{split}

where the supremum is taken over all
1
-bounded functions
u_{1},u_{2},g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{C}
.

Let us see how
9.4
is used to prove
9.3
.

Proof of
9.3
.

Since
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
and
f-f_{\mathrm{str}}=f_{\mathrm{unf}}
, we have for
g:\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]

\displaystyle\|f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}

\displaystyle g\,-\,f_{\mathrm{str}}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g\|_{2,[N_{s}]}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\Bigg|\Bigg(\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}f(n-m)g(m)\Bigg)-\Bigg(\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}f_{\mathrm{str}}(n-m)g(m)\Bigg)\Bigg|^{2}+{\rm o}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}f_{\mathrm{unf}}(n-m)g(m)\Bigg|^{2}+{\rm o}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}=1}^{H_{s}}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}g(m_{1})g(m_{2})f_{\mathrm{unf}}(n-m_{1})f_{\mathrm{unf}}(n-m_{2})+{\rm o}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}=1}^{H_{s}}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}g(m_{1})g(m_{2})f_{\mathrm{unf}}(n)f_{\mathrm{unf}}(n+m_{1}-m_{2})+{\rm o}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}=-H_{s}}^{H_{s}}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}g_{1}(m_{1})g_{2}(m_{2})f_{\mathrm{unf}}(n)f_{\mathrm{unf}}(n+m_{1}+m_{2})+{\rm o}(1),

where

g_{1}(m)=\begin{cases}g(m),&\text{if}\penalty\ m\geqslant 1,\\
0,&\text{if}\penalty\ m\leqslant 0,\end{cases}\hskip 20.00003pt\text{and}\hskip 20.00003ptg_{2}(m)=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if}\penalty\ m\geqslant 0,\\
g(-m),&\text{if}\penalty\ m\leqslant-1.\end{cases}

The claim now follows directly from
9.4
.
∎

It remains to prove
9.4
, for which we use the following lemmas.

Lemma 9.5
.

For any
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4},u_{1},u_{2}\colon\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{C}
with
\|f_{i}\|_{\infty},\|u_{j}\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1
we have

\Bigg|\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{n,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}u_{1}(h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})f_{1}(n)\overline{f_{2}(n+h_{1})}\overline{f_{3}(n+h_{2})}f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})\Bigg|\leqslant\|f_{4}\|_{U^{2}(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})}.

Proof.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{n,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}u_{1}(h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})f_{1}(n)\overline{f_{2}(n+h_{1})f_{3}(n+h_{2})}f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}u_{1}(h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2}+h_{3})f_{1}(n)\overline{f_{2}(n+h_{1})}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\overline{f_{3}(n+h_{2}+h_{3})}f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2}+h_{3})\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant\Bigg(\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{n,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{h_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}u_{2}(h_{2}+h_{3})\overline{f_{3}(n+h_{2}+h_{3})}f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2}+h_{3})\Bigg|^{2}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\frac{1}{N^{4}}\sum_{n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}u_{2}(h_{2})\overline{f_{3}(n+h_{2})}f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})\overline{u_{2}(h_{2}+h_{3})}f_{3}(n+h_{2}+h_{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\overline{f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2}+h_{3})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\frac{1}{N^{3}}\sum_{n,h_{1},h_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}u(h_{3})\overline{f_{3}(n)}f_{4}(n+h_{1})f_{3}(n+h_{3})\overline{f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{3})}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
u(h_{3})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{h_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}}u_{2}(h_{2})\overline{u_{2}(h_{2}+h_{3})}
. Repeating the same argument once more to remove the term
u(h_{3})
, the conclusion follows.
∎

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
H,N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varepsilon>0
with
\varepsilon^{2}N\leqslant H\leqslant\varepsilon N
. Then for any functions
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4},u_{1},u_{2}\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{C}
with
\|f_{i}\|_{\infty},\|u_{j}\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1
we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{H^{2}}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}=1}^{H}\Bigg(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}u_{1}(h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})f_{1}(n)\overline{f_{2}(n+h_{1})}\overline{f_{3}(n+h_{2})}

\displaystyle f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})\Bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant 64\varepsilon^{-4}\|f_{4}\|_{U^{2}([N])}+2\varepsilon.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{f}_{i}\colon\mathbb{Z}/4N\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{C}
denote the function
\tilde{f}_{i}(n)=f_{i}(n)
when
n\in[N]
and
\tilde{f}_{i}(n)=0
otherwise. Since
H\leqslant\varepsilon N
, we have uniformly over all
1\leqslant h_{1},h_{2}\leqslant H
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}u_{1}(h_{1})

\displaystyle u_{2}(h_{2})f_{1}(n)\overline{f_{2}(n+h_{1})}\overline{f_{3}(n+h_{2})}f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}/4N\mathbb{Z}}u_{1}(h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})\tilde{f}_{1}(n)\overline{\tilde{f}_{2}(n+h_{1})}\overline{\tilde{f}_{3}(n+h_{2})}\tilde{f}_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})\Bigg|\leqslant 2\varepsilon.

Hence

\displaystyle\Bigg|

\displaystyle\frac{1}{H^{2}}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}=1}^{H}\Bigg(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}u_{1}(h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})f_{1}(n)\overline{f_{2}(n+h_{1})}\overline{f_{3}(n+h_{2})}f_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})\Bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant\Bigg|\frac{1}{H^{2}}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}=1}^{H}\Bigg(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}/4N\mathbb{Z}}u_{1}(h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})\tilde{f}_{1}(n)\overline{\tilde{f}_{2}(n+h_{1})}\overline{\tilde{f}_{3}(n+h_{2})}\tilde{f}_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})\Bigg)\Bigg|+2\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\frac{64N^{2}}{H^{2}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{(4N)^{3}}\sum_{n,h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}/4N\mathbb{Z}}1_{[H]}(h_{1})u_{1}(h_{1})1_{[H]}(h_{2})u_{2}(h_{2})\tilde{f}_{1}(n)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\overline{\tilde{f}_{2}(n+h_{1})}\overline{\tilde{f}_{3}(n+h_{2})}\tilde{f}_{4}(n+h_{1}+h_{2})\Bigg|+2\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{64N^{2}}{H^{2}}\big\|\tilde{f}_{4}\big\|_{U^{2}(\mathbb{Z}/4N\mathbb{Z})}+2\varepsilon,

where the last inequality follows from
9.5
.
Using the assumption
\varepsilon^{2}N\leqslant H
and the fact
\big\|\tilde{f}_{4}\big\|_{U^{2}(\mathbb{Z}/4N\mathbb{Z})}\leqslant\big\|f_{4}\big\|_{U^{2}([N])}
, we obtain the desired bound and the proof is finished.
∎

Proof of
9.4
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
define the sequence
\mathbf{H}^{\prime}=(H_{s}^{\prime})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
via

H_{s}^{\prime}=\lfloor\varepsilon H_{s}\rfloor,\hskip 20.00003pt\forall s\in\mathbb{N}.

Since
\mathbf{H}^{\prime}\prec\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2}
, by dividing the averages of length
2H_{1,s}+1
and
2H_{2,s}+1
into averages of length
H_{s}^{\prime}
and using the triangle inequality, we get

\begin{split}\limsup_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\sup_{u_{1},u_{2},g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{H_{1,s}}\sum_{h_{1}=-H_{1,s}}^{H_{1,s}}\frac{1}{H_{2,s}}\sum_{h_{2}=-H_{2,s}}^{H_{2,s}}u_{1}(&h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})g_{1}(n)g_{2}(n+h_{1})\\
&\cdot g_{3}(n+h_{2})\overline{f(n+h_{1}+h_{2})}\Bigg|\\
\leqslant\limsup_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\sup_{u_{1},u_{2},g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{(H_{s}^{\prime})^{2}}\sum_{h_{1},h_{2}=1}^{H_{s}^{\prime}}u_{1}(&h_{1})u_{2}(h_{2})g_{1}(n)g_{2}(n+h_{1})\\
&\cdot g_{3}(n+h_{2})\overline{f(n+h_{1}+h_{2})}\Bigg|.\end{split}

Finally, dividing the average of length
N_{s}
into averages of length
H_{s}
and using
9.6
(applied with
H_{s}
in place of
N
and
H_{s}^{\prime}
in place of
H
), the last expression is bounded by

\leqslant 64\varepsilon^{-4}\Bigg(\limsup_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\|f\|_{U^{2}(\{n,n+1,\ldots,n+H_{s}-1\})}\Bigg)+2\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon
was arbitrary, we obtain the desired conclusion.
∎

9.3.  Almost-periods for
U^{2}(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
-structured functions

Theorem 9.7
.

Let
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]
, and suppose
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
are sequences in
\mathbb{N}
with

1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
.
Suppose
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
f
and
f=f_{\mathrm{str}}+f_{\mathrm{unf}}
is the associated splitting.
There exists a sequence
\varepsilon_{s}\to 0
such that for every
s\in\mathbb{N}
and every
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
we can find
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s})H_{s}
such that
Q_{n}
is highly divisible in the sense that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\min\{m\in\mathbb{N}:m\nmid Q_{n}\}=\infty,

and

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h)-f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h+Q_{n})|=0.

Proof of
9.7
.

We shall prove that for every fixed
\varepsilon>0
and
M\in\mathbb{N}
, we can find for every
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
a number
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon)H_{s}
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\min\{m\in\mathbb{N}:m\nmid Q_{n}\}\geqslant M,

and

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h)-f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h+Q_{n})|\leqslant\varepsilon.

It then follows that we may replace the fixed
\varepsilon
and
M
by sequences
\varepsilon_{s}\to 0
that tends to zero sufficiently slowly and
M_{s}\to\infty
that tends to infinity sufficiently slowly such that the desired conclusion still holds.

As
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
f
, there exist
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
c_{1},\ldots,c_{d}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{C}
with
\|c_{i}\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1
, and
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d}\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{R}
such that if
\mathcal{P}(n)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}c_{i}(n)e(n\alpha_{i}(n))
then

\|\mathcal{P}-f_{\mathrm{str}}\|_{2,\mathbf{N}}\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{3},

(9.1)

and for all
C\geqslant 1
if

\displaystyle\Gamma_{s}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\{n\in[N_{s}-H_{s}+1]:\alpha_{i}(n+m_{1})=\alpha_{i}(n+m_{2})\penalty\ \forall i\in[d],\penalty\ \forall m_{1},m_{2}\in[H_{s}]\},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{s}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\{n\in[N_{s}-H_{s}+1]:c_{i}(n+m_{1})=c_{i}(n+m_{2})\penalty\ \forall i\in[d],\penalty\ \forall m_{1},m_{2}\in[H_{s}]\},

\displaystyle W_{s,C}

\displaystyle=\Big\{n\in[N_{s}-H_{s}+1]:\text{for all}\penalty\ (w_{1},\ldots,w_{d})\in[-C,C]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d},\penalty\ \text{either}

\displaystyle\|w_{1}

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d}\alpha_{d}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{CH_{s}}\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \|w_{1}\alpha_{1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d}\alpha_{d}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\tfrac{C}{H_{s}}\Big\},

then

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|\Gamma_{s}^{(1)}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|\Gamma_{s}^{(2)}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|W_{s,C}|}{N_{s}-H_{s}+1}=1.

Since
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=\lim_{s\to\infty}H_{s}/N_{s}=0
, we can slightly modify the sets
\Gamma_{s}^{(1)}
,
\Gamma_{s}^{(2)}
and
W_{s,C}
by replacing
[N_{s}-H_{s}+1]
with
\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
and
H_{s}
with
3H_{s}
, so that if

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{s}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\{n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}:\alpha_{i}(n+m_{1})=\alpha_{i}(n+m_{2})\penalty\ \forall i\in[d],\penalty\ \forall m_{1},m_{2}\in[3H_{s}]\},

\displaystyle\tilde{\Gamma}_{s}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\{n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}:c_{i}(n+m_{1})=c_{i}(n+m_{2})\penalty\ \forall i\in[d],\penalty\ \forall m_{1},m_{2}\in[3H_{s}]\},

\displaystyle\tilde{W}_{s,C}

\displaystyle=\Big\{n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}:\text{for all}\penalty\ (w_{1},\ldots,w_{d})\in[-C,C]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d},\penalty\ \text{either}

\displaystyle\|w_{1}

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d}\alpha_{d}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{3CH_{s}}\penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \|w_{1}\alpha_{1}(n)+\ldots+w_{d}\alpha_{d}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\tfrac{C}{3H_{s}}\Big\},

then we still have

\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|\tilde{\Gamma}_{s}^{(1)}|}{N_{s}}=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|\tilde{\Gamma}_{s}^{(2)}|}{N_{s}}=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{|\tilde{W}_{s,C}|}{N_{s}}=1.

Now take
C=C(d,\frac{\varepsilon}{3d(M!)})
from
5.9
.
If
n\in\tilde{\Gamma}_{s}^{(1)}\cap\tilde{\Gamma}_{s}^{(2)}\cap\tilde{W}_{s,C}
then it follows from the conclusion of
5.9
, applied to
x=\frac{1}{3(M!)}+\frac{\varepsilon}{3d(M!)}
, that there exists some
Q^{\prime}_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
\frac{H_{s}}{M!}\leqslant Q^{\prime}_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon)\frac{H_{s}}{M!}
such that

\|Q^{\prime}_{n}\alpha_{i}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{3d(M!)},\hskip 20.00003pt\forall i=1,\ldots,d.

Let
Q_{n}=M!\cdot Q^{\prime}_{n}
.
Then
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon)H_{s}
,
M!\mid Q_{n}
, and

\|Q_{n}\alpha_{i}(n)\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{3d},\hskip 20.00003pt\forall i=1,\ldots,d.

Combined with the fact that
c_{i}(n+h)=c_{i}(n)
and
\alpha_{i}(n+h)=\alpha_{i}(n)
for all
h\in[3H_{s}]
, we conclude that

\max_{h\in[H_{s}]}|\mathcal{P}(n+h)-\mathcal{P}(n+h+Q_{n})|\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{3}.

(9.2)

If
n
does not belong to
\tilde{\Gamma}_{s}^{(1)}\cap\tilde{\Gamma}_{s}^{(2)}\cap\tilde{W}_{s,C}
for some
s
, then it does not matter what
Q_{n}
is, as the contribution of such
n
is negligible. For simplicity, let us take
Q_{n}=M!\cdot\lceil\frac{H_{s}}{M!}\rceil
in this case.
To finish the proof, we combine (
9.1
) and (
9.2
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h)-f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h+Q_{n})|

\displaystyle\leqslant\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|\mathcal{P}(n+h)-\mathcal{P}(n+h+Q_{n})|+\frac{2\varepsilon}{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\varepsilon,

arriving at the desired conclusion.
∎

Corollary 9.8
.

Let
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]
, and suppose
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
are sequences in
\mathbb{N}
with

1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
.
If
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
f
then
then there exists a sequence
\varepsilon_{s}\to 0
such that for all but
o(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, we can find
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s})H_{s}
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\min\{m\in\mathbb{N}:m\nmid Q_{n}\}=\infty

and

\sup_{g:\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h)-f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h+Q_{n})|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}.

Proof.

First, we use
9.7
to find a sequence
\varepsilon_{s}\to 0
such that for every
s\in\mathbb{N}
and every
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
we can find
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s})H_{s}
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\min\{m\in\mathbb{N}:m\nmid Q_{n}\}=\infty

and

\displaystyle\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h)-f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h+Q_{n})|=0.

(9.3)

In light of
9.3
, for every
g:\mathbb{N}\to[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}&\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h)-f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h+Q_{n})|\\
&=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f_{\mathrm{str}}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h)-f_{\mathrm{str}}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h+Q_{n})|+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1),\end{split}

(9.4)

where the
{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)
error term is independent of
g
.
Using the definition of the convolution of
f
with
g
at scale
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
, we get for
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}-3H_{s}\}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f_{\mathrm{str}}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h)-f_{\mathrm{str}}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h+Q_{n})|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}\Bigg|\Bigg(\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h-m)g(m)\Bigg)-\Bigg(\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h+Q_{n}-m)g(m)\Bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{H_{s}^{2}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}\big|f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h-m)-f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h+Q_{n}-m)\big|.

Since
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=\lim_{s\to\infty}H_{s}/N_{s}=0
, we therefore have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f_{\mathrm{str}}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h)-f_{\mathrm{str}}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h+Q_{n})|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}^{2}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}\big|f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h-m)-f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h+Q_{n}-m)\big|+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}\big|f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h)-f_{\mathrm{str}}(n+h+Q_{n})\big|+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)

Combined with (
9.3
) and (
9.4
), this now gives

\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h)-f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h+Q_{n})|={\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1).

Replacing
\varepsilon_{s}
with a more slowly decaying sequence if necessary, we get

\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h)-f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h+Q_{n})|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}.

In other words, for all but
o(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
we have

\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h)-f*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}g(n+h+Q_{n})|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}.

as desired.
∎

9.4.  Modulated
\delta
-popular sumsets at intermediate scales

Given
Q\in\mathbb{N}
,
E,F\subseteq\{0,1,\ldots,Q-1\}
, and
\delta>0
, we define
E+_{\delta,\bmod{Q}}F
to be the
\delta
-popular sumset (as defined in (
4.2
)) of
E
and
F
viewed as subsets of
\mathbb{Z}/Q\mathbb{Z}
with addition taken modulo
Q
.
Observe that

\displaystyle E+_{\delta,\bmod{Q}}F=\bigg\{h\in\{0,1,\ldots,Q-1\}:\frac{1}{Q}

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{h}1_{E}(h-m)1_{F}(m)

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\frac{1}{Q}\sum_{m=h+1}^{Q-1}1_{E}(h+Q-m)1_{F}(m)>\delta\bigg\}.

Theorem 9.9
.

Let
A\subseteq\mathbb{N}
such that
d(A)>0
, and let
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be sequences in
\mathbb{N}
with

1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
.
If
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
A
then there exist sequences
\varepsilon_{s}\to 0
and
\delta_{s}\to 0
such that for all but
o(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, there exists
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s})H_{s}
such that
Q_{n}
is highly divisible in the sense that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\min\{m\in\mathbb{N}:m\nmid Q_{n}\}=\infty,

and for every
\tilde{B}_{s}\subseteq[H_{s}]
,

\left|\Big(\big((A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\big)+_{\delta_{s},\bmod{Q_{n}}}\tilde{B}_{s}\Big)\right.\\
\left.\backslash\Big((A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\Big)\right|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}.

Proof.

Using
9.8
, we can find a sequence
\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}\to 0
and sets
G_{s}\subseteq\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
with
|G_{s}|\geqslant 1-{\rm o}(N_{s})
such that for every
n\in G_{s}
there exists some highly divisible
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime})H_{s}
and

\sup_{B\subseteq\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{h=0}^{H_{s}-1}|1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{B}(n+h)-1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{B}(n+Q_{n}+h)|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}.

This implies that

\sup_{B\subseteq\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{h=0}^{Q_{n}-1}|1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{B}(n+h)-1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{B}(n+Q_{n}+h)|\leqslant 4\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}.

Fix a set
\tilde{B}_{s}\subseteq[H_{s}]
, and let
D_{n}
denote the set of all
h\in\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
such that

|1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(n+h)-1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(n+Q_{n}+h)|\geqslant\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}}/4.

By Markov’s inequality we have
|D_{n}|\leqslant 16\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}}Q_{n}
.
We claim that for appropriately chosen
\delta_{s}>0
(which we will choose independently of the set
\tilde{B}_{s}
),

\Big(\big((A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\big)+_{\delta_{s},\bmod{Q_{n}}}\tilde{B}_{s}\Big)\\
\backslash\Big((A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\Big)\subseteq D_{n}.

Indeed, if
h\in\big((A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\big)+_{\delta_{s},\bmod{Q_{n}}}\tilde{B}_{s}
then

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{m=0}^{h}1_{(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}}(h-m)1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(m)\\
+\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{m=h+1}^{Q_{n}-1}1_{(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}}(h+Q_{n}-m)1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(m)>\delta_{s}.

We can replace
(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
with
(A-n)
, since the ranges of the sums guarantee that we are in
\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
.
Therefore,

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{m=0}^{h}1_{(A-n)}(h-m)1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(m)+\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{m=h+1}^{Q_{n}-1}1_{(A-n)}(h+Q_{n}-m)1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(m)>\delta_{s}.

If additionally,
h\notin(A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
then we have

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{m=0}^{h}1_{(A-n)}(h-m)1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(m)=0

and hence

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{m=h+1}^{Q_{n}-1}1_{(A-n)}(h+Q_{n}-m)1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(m)>\delta_{s},

which implies

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\sum_{m=1}^{Q_{n}}1_{(A-n)}(h+Q_{n}-m)1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(m)>\delta_{s}.

Using the assumption
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime})H_{s}
once more gives

\frac{1}{H_{s}}\sum_{m=1}^{H_{s}}1_{(A-n)}(h+Q_{n}-m)1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(m)>\delta_{s}-3\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}.

Using the definition of the convolution at scale
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
, we can rewrite the last inequality as

1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(n+Q_{n}+h)>\delta_{s}-3\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}.

Since
h\notin(A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
implies
h\notin(A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
and therefore
1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(n+h)=0
, we conclude that

|1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(n+h)-1_{A}*_{(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})}1_{\tilde{B}_{s}}(n+Q_{n}+h)|>\delta_{s}-3\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}.

Taking
\delta_{s}=\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}}/4+3\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}
, we get that

h\in D_{n}
as claimed. Finally, we pick
\varepsilon_{s}=16\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}^{\prime}}
then
|D_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
, completing the proof.
∎

9.5.  Modulated sumsets at intermediate scales

We are now in position to prove
9.1
.
The proof consists primarily of taking
9.9
and then applying
4.7
to eliminate the usage of
\delta_{s}
appearing in
9.9
.

Proof of
9.1
.

Suppose
A\subseteq\mathbb{N}
with
d(A)>0
, and let
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be sequences with
1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
.
Assume also that
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
A
.
In light of
9.9
, there exist sequences
\varepsilon_{1,s}\to 0
and
\delta_{s}\to 0
such that for all but
o(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, there exists highly divisible
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{1,s})H_{s}
such that for every
\tilde{B}_{s}\subseteq[H_{s}]
,

\left|\Big(\big((A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\big)+_{\delta_{s},\bmod{Q_{n}}}\tilde{B}_{s}\Big)\right.\\
\left.\backslash\Big((A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\Big)\right|\leqslant\varepsilon_{1,s}Q_{n}.

(9.5)

Now choose a sequence
\varepsilon_{2,s}\to 0
such that
\delta^{\prime}_{s}=\delta(\varepsilon_{2,s})
as given by
4.7
satisfies
\delta^{\prime}_{s}\geqslant\delta_{s}
.
For each such
n
, we then apply
4.7
to the finite group
\mathbb{Z}/Q_{n}\mathbb{Z}
in order to find sets
A_{n}\subseteq(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
and
B_{n}\subseteq\tilde{B}_{s}
such that:

|(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash A_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{2,s}Q_{n},\hskip 20.00003pt|\tilde{B}_{s}\backslash B_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{2,s}Q_{n},

and

\Big|(A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n})\backslash\Big(\big((A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\big)+_{\delta_{s},\bmod{Q_{n}}}\tilde{B}_{s}\Big)\Big|\leqslant\varepsilon_{2,s}Q_{n}.

Combining the last part with (
9.5
), we get

\left|\big(A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}\big)\backslash\big((A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\big)\right|\leqslant(\varepsilon_{1,s}+\varepsilon_{2,s})Q_{n}.

Putting
\varepsilon_{s}=2(\varepsilon_{1,s}+\varepsilon_{2,s})\to 0
, we have

\left|\big(A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}\big)\backslash\big((A+\tilde{B}_{s}-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\big)\right|\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon_{s}}{2}Q_{n}.

(9.6)

Finally, if
\tilde{B}_{s}=B\cap[H_{s}]
, we can replace
B_{n}
with
B_{n}\cup(B\cap[J_{s}])
for some non-decreasing slow-growing sequence
J_{s}\to\infty
.
If the sequence
(J_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
grows sufficiently slowly, then the error introduced by this modification can be absorbed by replacing
\tfrac{\varepsilon_{s}}{2}
with
\varepsilon_{s}
in (
9.6
).
Consequently, if
B
meets every residue class in
\mathbb{N}
, then, since the sets
B_{n}
contain increasingly large initial segments of
B
, we conclude that there exists a slowly growing sequence
D_{s}\to\infty
such that each
B_{n}
can be chosen to have non-empty intersection with every residue class modulo
m
for all
m\leqslant D_{s}
.
∎

10.  A local inverse theorem for sumsets of sets of positive density

The aim of this section is to prove a “local” version of
1.4
, extracting a local structural description of sets
A
and
B
for which
\underline{d}(A+B)=d(A)+d(B)
.
The strategy is to use
9.1
to relate the local properties of the sumset
A+B
to a sumset in a cyclic group and then apply the inverse theorem for sumsets in cyclic groups expressed in
4.6
.

Theorem 10.1
.

Let
A,B\subseteq\mathbb{N}
with
d(A)=\alpha>0
and
d(B)=\beta>0
such that
\alpha+\beta<1
.
Suppose
B
meets every residue class in
\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be an increasing sequence with
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
such that
d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)=\alpha+\beta
.
Let
\mathbf{H}_{G}=(H_{G,s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}_{G}\prec\mathbf{N}
be a Gowers scale for
A
as guaranteed by
8.5
(ii)
.
Let
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be a
U^{2}
good scale for
A
with
\mathbf{H}_{G}\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
.
Then, after passing to a subsequence of
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
, there exists
H=h\mathbb{Z}
for some
h\in\mathbb{N}
and a sequences
k_{s},C_{s}\to\infty
such that for all
s\in\mathbb{N}
and all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, there exists a decomposition

(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}=(A_{n}\cup E_{n})-a_{n}

and

B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}=(B_{s,0}\cup B_{s,1}\cup F_{s})-b_{s}

such that
A_{n},B_{s,0}\subseteq H
,
a_{n},b_{s}\in\{0,1,\ldots,h-1\}
,
B_{s,1}\subseteq\mathbb{N}\backslash H
with
|B_{s,1}|=\left(1-\frac{1}{h}-{\rm o}(1)\right)H_{s}
,
|E_{n}|={\rm o}(H_{s})
,
|F_{s}|={\rm o}(H_{s})
, and one of the following two conditions is satisfied:

(1)

There exists
\theta_{s}\in\mathbb{T}
with
\min_{q\in[k_{s}]}\|q\theta_{s}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\frac{C_{s}}{H_{s}}
and closed intervals
I_{n},J_{s}\subseteq\mathbb{T}
such that if
\phi_{s}:H\to\mathbb{T}
is the map
\phi_{s}(m)=m\theta_{s}
for
m\in H
, then

A_{n}\subseteq\phi_{s}^{-1}(I_{n}),\hskip 10.00002ptB_{s,0}\subseteq\phi_{s}^{-1}(J_{s}),

and

|\phi_{s}^{-1}(I_{n})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash A_{n}|,|\phi_{s}^{-1}(J_{s})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash B_{s,0}|={\rm o}(H_{s}).

(2)

|B_{s,0}|={\rm o}(H_{s})
and
|A_{n}+B_{s,0}|=|A_{n}|+{\rm o}(H_{s})
.

Proof.

We combine
9.1
and
4.6
.

By
9.1
, there exist sequences
\varepsilon_{s}\to 0
and
D_{s}\to\infty
such that for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, we can find
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+\varepsilon_{s})H_{s}
,
A_{n}\subseteq(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
and
B_{n}\subseteq B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
such that

(I)

|(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash A_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
,

(II)

|B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash B_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
,

(III)

|(A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n})\backslash((A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\})|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}
, and

(IV)

B_{n}
intersects every residue class mod
m\leqslant D_{s}
.

Since
\mathbf{H}\succ\mathbf{H}_{G}
, we also have that for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
,

\displaystyle|(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}|

\displaystyle=(\alpha+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n}

and

\displaystyle|B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}|

\displaystyle=(\beta+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n},

so
|A_{n}|=(\alpha+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n}
and
|B_{n}|=(\beta+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{n}
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1-\alpha-\beta)
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
be given.
Put
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\eta=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, and let
\delta=\delta(\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime},\eta,k)>0
and
D=D(\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime},\eta,k)\in\mathbb{N}
be given by
4.6
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Note that since
D_{s}\to\infty
, we have
D_{s}\geqslant D
for all large
s
, so
4.6
applies to the pair
(A_{n},B_{n})
.
We consider several cases.

We first rule out the possibility that conditions
(i)
or
(ii)
from
4.6
hold for a positive proportion of
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
.
Suppose for contradiction that there exists
c>0
such that conclusion
(i)
or
(ii)
from
4.6
holds for infinitely many
s\in\mathbb{N}
and at least
cN_{s}
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
.
We pass to a subsequence to upgrade from “infinitely many”
s\in\mathbb{N}
to “every”
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then for every
s\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a set
L_{s}\subseteq\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
with
|L_{s}|\geqslant cN_{s}
with the property that if
n\in L_{s}
, then

|A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}|\geqslant\min\left\{(1-\varepsilon)Q_{n},(\alpha+\beta+\delta)Q_{n}\right\}=(\alpha+\beta+\lambda)Q_{n},

where
\lambda=\min\{\delta,1-\varepsilon-\alpha-\beta\}>0
.
Note that by
4.4
and
(IV)
, we also have

|A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}|\geqslant|A_{n}|+|B_{n}|-D_{s}^{-1}Q_{n}

for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
.
Therefore, applying
(III)
, we have

\displaystyle d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{|A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}|}{Q_{n}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\left(\sum_{n\in L_{s}}\frac{|A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}|}{Q_{n}}+\sum_{n\notin L_{s}}\frac{|A_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}B_{n}|}{Q_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\left(|L_{s}|(\alpha+\beta+\lambda)+(N_{s}-|L_{s}|)(\alpha+\beta-D_{s}^{-1})\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant c(\alpha+\beta+\lambda)+(1-c)(\alpha+\beta)

\displaystyle=\alpha+\beta+c\lambda>\alpha+\beta.

This is a contradiction, so the set of
n
for which conclusion
(i)
or
(ii)
holds has size
{\rm o}(N_{s})
.
In other words, for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, either conclusion
(iii)
or conclusion
(iv)
holds.

We now analyze the consequences of
(iii)
and
(iv)
.
Suppose
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
and
(iii)
holds.
That is, there exists
h_{n}\leqslant D
with
h_{n}\mid Q_{n}
,
a_{n},b_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,h_{n}-1\}
, and a decomposition
A_{n}=A^{\prime}_{n}-a_{n}
and
B_{n}=(B^{\prime}_{n,0}\cup B^{\prime}_{n,1})-b_{n}
such that

(iii.a)

A^{\prime}_{n},B^{\prime}_{n,0}\subseteq h_{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
,

(iii.b)

B^{\prime}_{n,1}\subseteq\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash h_{n}\mathbb{N}
and
|B^{\prime}_{n,1}|>\left(1-\frac{1}{h_{n}}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2h_{n}}\right)Q_{n}
, and

(iii.c)

there exists
t_{n},N_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
N_{n}>k
,
\gcd(t_{n},N_{n})=1
, and
N_{n}\mid\frac{Q_{n}}{h_{n}}
, and there exist intervals
I_{n},J_{n}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}/N_{n}\mathbb{Z}
such that if
\phi_{n}:\left\{0,h_{n},2h_{n},\ldots,Q_{n}-h_{n}\right\}\to\mathbb{Z}/N_{n}\mathbb{Z}
is given by
\phi_{n}(h_{n}m)=mt_{n}
, then

A^{\prime}_{n}\subseteq\phi_{n}^{-1}(I_{n}),\hskip 10.00002ptB^{\prime}_{n,0}\subseteq\phi_{n}^{-1}(J_{n}),\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt|\phi_{n}^{-1}(I_{n})\backslash A^{\prime}_{n}|,|\phi_{n}^{-1}(J_{n})\backslash B^{\prime}_{n,0}|<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}Q_{n}.

Embedding
\mathbb{Z}/N_{n}\mathbb{Z}
as the subgroup
\{0,\frac{1}{N_{n}},\ldots,\frac{N_{n}-1}{N_{n}}\}\subseteq\mathbb{T}
and letting
\theta_{n}=\frac{t_{n}}{h_{n}N_{n}}\in\mathbb{T}
, we can find intervals
\tilde{I}_{n},\tilde{J}_{n}\subseteq\mathbb{T}
such that if
\tilde{\phi}_{n}:\{0,h_{n},2h_{n},\ldots,Q_{n}-h_{n}\}\to\mathbb{T}
is given by
\tilde{\phi}_{n}(m)=m\theta_{n}
, then

A^{\prime}_{n}\subseteq\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{I}_{n}),\hskip 10.00002ptB^{\prime}_{n,0}\subseteq\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{J}_{n}),\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\left|\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{I}_{n})\backslash A^{\prime}_{n}\right|,\left|\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{J}_{n})\backslash B^{\prime}_{n,0}\right|<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}Q_{n}.

Since
k<N_{n}\leqslant Q_{n}
, we also have
\|q\theta\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\frac{1}{N_{n}}\geqslant\frac{1}{Q_{n}}
for
q\in[k]
.

Note that by
(iii.b)
,

|B_{n}|\geqslant|B^{\prime}_{n,1}|\geqslant Q_{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{h_{n}}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2h_{n}}\right),

so

h_{n}\leqslant\frac{1+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}{1-\beta-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)}\leqslant\frac{2}{1-\beta}

(10.1)

for all large enough
s
.

Combining
(iii.c)
with
(I)
and applying the triangle inequality, we have

\left|\left((A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\right)\triangle\left(\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{I}_{n})-a_{n}\right)\right|\\
\leqslant\left|\left((A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\right)\triangle A_{n}\right|+\left|A^{\prime}_{n}\triangle\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{I}_{n})\right|\leqslant\left(\varepsilon_{s}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)Q_{n}.

Let
E_{n}=\left((A-n+a_{n})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\right)\triangle\left(\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{I}_{n})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\right)
and
F_{n}=B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash B_{n}
.
Put
\tilde{A}_{n}=A^{\prime}_{n}\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
and
\tilde{B}_{n,i}=B^{\prime}_{n,i}\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
for
i\in\{0,1\}
.
This provides a decomposition

(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}=(\tilde{A}_{n}\cup E_{n})-a_{n}

and

B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}=(\tilde{B}_{n,0}\cup\tilde{B}_{n,1}\cup F_{n})-b_{n}

with the property that (as long as
s\in\mathbb{N}
is sufficiently large)

(a)

|\tilde{B}_{n,1}|\geqslant Q_{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{h_{n}}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2h_{n}}\right)-(Q_{n}-H_{s})>H_{s}\left(1-\frac{1}{h_{n}}-\varepsilon\right)
,

(b)

|E_{n}|\leqslant\left(\varepsilon_{s}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)Q_{n}<\varepsilon H_{s}
,

(c)

|F_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}<\varepsilon H_{s}
, and

(d)

there exists
\theta_{n}\in\mathbb{T}
with
\min_{q\in[k]}\|q\theta_{n}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2H_{s}}
and intervals
\tilde{I}_{n},\tilde{J}_{n}\subseteq\mathbb{T}
such that if
\tilde{\phi}_{n}:h_{n}\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{T}
is given by
\tilde{\phi}_{n}(m)=m\theta_{n}
, then

\tilde{A}_{n}\subseteq\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{I}_{n}),\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\tilde{B}_{n,0}\subseteq\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{J}_{n}),

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{I}_{n})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash\tilde{A}_{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}Q_{n}<\varepsilon H_{s}

and

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\phi}_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{J}_{n})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash\tilde{B}_{n,0}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}Q_{n}<\varepsilon H_{s}.

Let us observe that since
(\mathbf{N},\mathbf{H})
is a
U^{2}
good scale for
A
and the frequency
\theta_{n}
satisfies
\min_{q\in[k]}\|q\theta_{n}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\frac{1}{2H_{s}}
, the dichotomy provided by condition
(ii)
in
8.6
(see the definition of the set
W_{s,C}
) implies that for a given
C\geqslant 1
, we have
\min_{q\in[k]}\|q\theta_{n}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\frac{C}{H_{s}}
if
s
is sufficiently large in terms of
C
and
k
.
Hence, we in fact have

(d’)

\min_{q\in[k]}\|q\theta_{n}\|_{\mathbb{T}}\geqslant\frac{C_{s}}{H_{s}}
for some sequence
C_{s}\to\infty
.

When
(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
and
B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
admit decompositions satisfying
(a)
–
(d)
and
(d’)
, we will say that
(A,B)
satisfies property
(1_{h_{n},\varepsilon,k})
at
n
.

Now suppose
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
and
(iv)
holds.
Then there exists
h_{n}\leqslant D
with
h_{n}\mid D
, elements
a_{n},b_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,h-1\}
, and a decomposition
A_{n}=A^{\prime}_{n}-a_{n}
and
B_{n}=(B^{\prime}_{n,0}\cup B^{\prime}_{n,1})-b_{n}
such that

(iv.a)

A^{\prime}_{n},B^{\prime}_{n,0}\subseteq h_{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
,

(iv.b)

B^{\prime}_{n,1}\subseteq\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash h\mathbb{N}
and
|B^{\prime}_{n,1}|>\left(1-\frac{1}{h_{n}}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2h_{n}}\right)Q_{n}
, and

(iv.c)

|B^{\prime}_{n,0}|<\frac{\varepsilon}{2h_{n}}Q_{n}
.

From condition
(iv.b)
, we conclude
h_{n}\leqslant\frac{2}{1-\beta}
as in the previous case.
Also as above, we may define
\tilde{A}_{n}=A^{\prime}_{n}\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
,
\tilde{B}_{n,i}=B^{\prime}_{n,i}\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
for
i\in\{0,1\}
,
E_{n}=(A-n+a_{n})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash\tilde{A}_{n}
, and
F_{n}=B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash B_{n}
.
Then

(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}=(\tilde{A}_{n}\cup E_{n})-a_{n}

and

B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}=(\tilde{B}_{n,0}\cup\tilde{B}_{n,1}\cup F_{n})-b_{n},

and this decomposition satisfies (for large enough
s
)

(a)

\tilde{A}_{n},\tilde{B}_{n,0}\subseteq h_{n}\mathbb{Z}\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
,

(b)

\tilde{B}_{n,1}\subseteq\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash h_{n}\mathbb{Z}
and

|\tilde{B}_{n,1}|\geqslant\left(1-\frac{1}{h_{n}}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2h_{n}}\right)Q_{n}-(Q_{n}-H_{s})>\left(1-\frac{1}{h_{n}}-\varepsilon\right)H_{s},

(c)

|E_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}<\varepsilon H_{s}
,

(d)

|F_{n}|\leqslant\varepsilon_{s}Q_{n}<\varepsilon H_{s}
, and

(e)

|\tilde{B}_{n,0}|\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2h_{n}}Q_{n}<\varepsilon H_{s}
.

When we have such a decomposition, we say that
(A,B)
satisfies property
(2_{h_{n},\varepsilon})
at
n
.

We have shown that for every
\varepsilon>0
, every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, every
s\in\mathbb{N}
, and all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, the pair
(A,B)
satisfies either property
(1_{h_{n},\varepsilon,k})
or
(2_{h_{n},\varepsilon})
for some
h_{n}\leqslant\frac{2}{1-\beta}
.
Now taking
\varepsilon=\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}
with
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}\to 0
and
k=k_{s}
with
k_{s}\to\infty
sufficiently slowly, we have that for every
s\in\mathbb{N}
and all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, the pair
(A,B)
satisfies either property
(1_{h_{n},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s},k_{s}})
or
(2_{h_{n},\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}})
for some
h_{n}\leqslant\frac{2}{1-\beta}
.

Both of the properties
(1_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s},k_{s}})
and
(2_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}})
provide decompositions of
B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}
.
The properties corresponding to different values of
h
are mutually incompatible, so there can be at most one value of
h
for each
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
By the pigeonhole principle, we may pick
h\in\mathbb{N}
and pass to a subsequence such that for every
s\in\mathbb{N}
and all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, the pair
(A,B)
satisfies either property
(1_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s},k_{s}})
or
(2_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}})
at
n
.

Suppose that for arbitrarily large
s\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
n_{s}\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
such that
(A,B)
satisfies property
(2_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}})
at
n_{s}
.
By passing to a subsequence, we may assume
n_{s}
exists for every
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then necessarily
\beta=1-\frac{1}{h}
, since
|B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}|=(\beta+{\rm o}(1))H_{s}
.
For
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
for which
(A,B)
satisfies property
(1_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s},k_{s}})
, we then also have that
(A,B)
satisfies property
(2_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}})
: the decomposition of
B
at
n_{s}
using property
(2_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}})
combined with the decomposition of
A
at
n
using property
(1_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s},k_{s}})
provides a decomposition of
(A,B)
at
n
satisfying property
(2_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s}})
.
Thus, property
(2)
holds by taking
B_{s,0},B_{s,1},F_{s}
, and
b_{s}
according to the decomposition of
B
at
n_{s}
.

If the hypothesis of the previous paragraph fails, then for every
s\in\mathbb{N}
and all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, we have that
(A,B)
satisfies property
(1_{h,\varepsilon^{\prime}_{s},k_{s}})
at
n
.
This verifies property
(1)
, with the caveat that property
(1)
requires a decomposition of
B
and values of
\theta_{s}
depending only on
s
and not on
n
.
However, since all of the decompositions of
B
for different values of
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
are representing the same set, we may adjust the decompositions with only negligible changes in order to make the decomposition depend only on
s
.
∎

11.  From local residue classes to global residue classes

The goal of this section is to prove the following proposition, which takes the local information about sets
A
and
B
at scale
\mathbf{H}
as given by conclusion
(2)
in
10.1
and converts it into global information at scale
\mathbf{N}
to derive condition
(2)
in
1.4
.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
A,B\subseteq\mathbb{N}
with
d(A)=\alpha>0
and
d(B)=\beta>0
such that
\alpha+\beta<1
.
Suppose
B
meets every residue class in
\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be an increasing sequence with
\lim_{s\to\infty}N_{s}/N_{s+1}=0
such that
d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)=\alpha+\beta
.
Let
\mathbf{H}_{G,A}=(H_{G,A,s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}_{G,A}\prec\mathbf{N}
be a Gowers scale for
A
as guaranteed by
8.5
(ii)
and
\mathbf{H}_{G,B}=(H_{G,B,s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}_{G,B}\prec\mathbf{N}
a Gowers scale for
B
.
Let
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be a
U^{2}
good scale for
A
and
B
with
\mathbf{H}_{G,A},\mathbf{H}_{G,B}\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
.
Suppose there exist
h\in\mathbb{N}
and
b_{0}\in\{0,1,\ldots,h-1\}
such that

•

for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, there exists
a_{n}\in\{0,1,\ldots,h-1\}
such that
(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}=(A_{n}\cup E_{n})-a_{n}
for some
A_{n}\subseteq h\mathbb{Z}
and
|E_{n}|={\rm o}(H_{s})
, and

•

B\sim_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{N}\backslash h\mathbb{N})-b_{0}
.

Then there exists
a_{0}\in\{0,1,\ldots,h-1\}
such that
A\subseteq h\mathbb{N}-a_{0}
,
A+h\sim_{\mathbf{N}}A
, and
B\sim_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathbb{N}\backslash h\mathbb{N})-b_{0}
.

In the proof, we will use the following combinatorial lemma.

Lemma 11.2
.

Let
h,q\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\alpha\in\left(0,\frac{1}{qh}\right)
, and let
\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{h-1}\in[0,1]
such that
\frac{1}{h}\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}\beta_{j}=1-\frac{1}{qh}
.
If
\min_{0\leqslant j\leqslant h-1}\beta_{j}\geqslant 1-\frac{1}{q}+\eta
for some
\eta>0
, then

\frac{1}{h}\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}\min\{h\alpha+\beta_{j},1\}\geqslant\min\left\{1,2\alpha+1-\frac{1}{qh},\alpha+1-\frac{1}{qh}+\frac{\eta}{h}\right\}.

Proof.

Let
J=\{0\leqslant j\leqslant h-1:h\alpha+\beta_{j}\leqslant 1\}
.
Then

\frac{1}{h}\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}\min\{h\alpha+\beta_{j},1\}=\frac{1}{h}\left(\sum_{j\in J}(h\alpha+\beta_{j})+\sum_{j\notin J}1\right)=|J|\alpha+1-\frac{1}{qh}+\frac{1}{h}\sum_{j\notin J}(1-\beta_{j}).

If
|J|\geqslant 2
, then

\frac{1}{h}\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}\min\{h\alpha+\beta_{j},1\}\geqslant 2\alpha+1-\frac{1}{qh},

and we are done.

We check the remaining two cases.
First, if
J=\emptyset
, then

\frac{1}{h}\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}\min\{h\alpha+\beta_{j},1\}=1,

in which case we are again done.

Finally, suppose
|J|=1
.
By symmetry, we may assume
J=\{0\}
.
Now,

\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}\beta_{j}=h-\frac{1}{q}-\beta_{0}\leqslant h-1-\eta,

so

\frac{1}{h}\sum_{j\notin J}(1-\beta_{j})\geqslant\frac{h-1}{h}-\frac{1}{h}\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}\beta_{j}\geqslant\frac{\eta}{h}.

∎

We will also make use of the following variant of
4.3
.

Lemma 11.3
.

Let
A\subseteq\mathbb{N}
.
If
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of natural numbers such that
N_{s}\to\infty
and there exists another sequence
\mathbf{H}=(H_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
1\prec\mathbf{H}\prec\mathbf{N}
such that

d(A,[N_{s}])\geqslant\alpha+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)

and

\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}|d(A,[N_{s}])-d\left(A,\{n+1,\ldots,n+H_{s}\}\right)|={\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1),

then there exists a sequence of natural numbers
(n_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\lim_{s\to\infty}n_{s}/N_{s}=0
and such that

\sigma(A,\{n_{s}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\})\geqslant\alpha+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1).

Proof.

Let
\alpha_{s}=d(A,[N_{s}])\geqslant\alpha+{\rm o}(1)
, and let

\delta_{s}=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\left|d\left(A,\{n+1,\ldots,n+H_{s}\}\right)-\alpha_{s}\right|={\rm o}(1).

Let
M_{s}\leqslant N_{s}
and
\varepsilon_{s}>0
be parameters to be chosen later.
Put
\gamma_{s}=d(A,[M_{s}])
.
By Lemma
4.3
, there exists
x\leqslant(1-\varepsilon_{s})M_{s}
such that
\sigma(A,\{x+1,\ldots,M_{s}\})\geqslant\gamma_{s}-\varepsilon_{s}
.
Let
y\in\{M_{s}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
.
Then

\displaystyle|A\cap\{x+1,\ldots,y\}|

\displaystyle=|A\cap\{x+1,\ldots,M_{s}\}|+|A\cap\{M_{s}+1,\ldots,y\}|

\displaystyle\geqslant(\gamma_{s}-\varepsilon_{s})(M_{s}-x)+\sum_{n=M_{s}+1}^{y}d(A,\{n+1,\ldots,n+H_{s}\})+{\rm O}(H_{s})

\displaystyle\geqslant(\gamma_{s}-\varepsilon_{s})(M_{s}-x)+\alpha_{s}(y-M_{s})

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt-\sum_{n=M_{s}+1}^{y}\left|d(A,\{n+1,\ldots,n+H_{s}\})-\alpha_{s}\right|+{\rm O}(H_{s})

\displaystyle\geqslant(\gamma_{s}-\varepsilon_{s})(M_{s}-x)+\alpha_{s}(y-M_{s})-\delta_{s}N_{s}+{\rm O}(H_{s}).

We now select the parameters to achieve the desired outcome.
In order to overcome the
\delta_{s}N_{s}
term, we should choose
M_{s}
large compared with
\delta_{s}N_{s}
and so that
\gamma_{s}\geqslant\alpha_{s}+{\rm o}(1)
.
On the other hand, we want
n_{s}=x={\rm o}(N_{s})
, so
M_{s}
cannot be too large.
Put
M_{s}=\max\{\sqrt{\delta_{s}}N_{s},\sqrt{H_{s}N_{s}}\}
.
Note that

\gamma_{s}=d(A,[M_{s}])=\frac{1}{M_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{M_{s}}d(A,\{n+1,\ldots,n+H_{s}\})+{\rm O}\left(\frac{H_{s}}{M_{s}}\right)\\
\geqslant\alpha_{s}-\frac{\delta_{s}N_{s}}{M_{s}}+{\rm O}\left(\frac{H_{s}}{M_{s}}\right)\geqslant\alpha_{s}-\sqrt{\delta_{s}}+{\rm O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{H_{s}}{N_{s}}}\right)=\alpha_{s}+{\rm o}(1).

Thus, for
y\in\{M_{s}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
,

\displaystyle d(A,\{x+1,\ldots,y\})

\displaystyle\geqslant\alpha_{s}-\varepsilon_{s}+{\rm o}(1)-\frac{\delta_{s}N_{s}}{y-x}+{\rm O}\left(\frac{H_{s}}{y-x}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\alpha_{s}-\varepsilon_{s}-\frac{\delta_{s}N_{s}}{\varepsilon_{s}M_{s}}+{\rm O}\left(\frac{H_{s}}{\varepsilon_{s}M_{s}}\right)+{\rm o}(1)

\displaystyle\geqslant\alpha_{s}-\varepsilon_{s}-\varepsilon_{s}^{-1}\sqrt{\delta_{s}}+{\rm O}\left(\varepsilon_{s}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{H_{s}}{N_{s}}}\right)+{\rm o}(1).

The quantities
\sqrt{\delta_{s}}
and
\sqrt{\frac{H_{s}}{N_{s}}}
both tend to zero as
s\to\infty
, so we can choose
\varepsilon_{s}={\rm o}(1)
going to zero sufficiently slowly so that

\varepsilon_{s}^{-1}\sqrt{\delta_{s}}={\rm o}(1)

and

\varepsilon_{s}^{-1}\sqrt{\frac{H_{s}}{N_{s}}}={\rm o}(1).

For example, we can take

\varepsilon_{s}=\max\left\{\delta_{s}^{1/4},\left(\frac{H_{s}}{N_{s}}\right)^{1/4}\right\}.

Putting everything together and taking
n_{s}=x
, we have
n_{s}\leqslant M_{s}={\rm o}(N_{s})
and

\sigma(A,\{n_{s}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\})\geqslant\min\left\{\sigma(A,\{n_{s}+1,\ldots,M_{s}\}),\min_{M_{s}<y\leqslant N_{s}}d(A,\{n_{s}+1,\ldots,y\})\right\}\\
\geqslant\alpha_{s}+{\rm o}(1)\geqslant\alpha+{\rm o}(1).

∎

Proof of Proposition
11.1
.

As a preliminary remark, we note that the condition
B\sim_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{N}\backslash h\mathbb{N})-b_{0}
implies that
\beta=d(B)=d_{\mathbf{H}}(B)=1-\frac{1}{h}
.

We will deduce the global information about
A
and
B
in stages.
First we show that
A+h\sim_{\mathbf{N}}A
.
Then we show that in almost every subinterval of length
H_{s}
in
\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
,
B
is approximately the union of
h-1
residue classes mod
h
.
This local description of
B
allows us to conclude globally that
A
belongs to a single residue class mod
h
.
Hence, taking
B_{0}=(B+b_{0})\cap h\mathbb{N}
and
B_{1}=(B+b_{0})\backslash h\mathbb{N}
, we can write
A+B
as a disjoint union of
A+B_{0}
and
A+B_{1}
.
Finally, we leverage this disjointness to show that the
d_{\mathbf{N}}(B_{0})=0
.

Let
B_{0}=(B+b_{0})\cap h\mathbb{N}
, and let
b_{1},b_{2}\in B_{0}
with
b_{1}<b_{2}
.
Then applying
9.1
, for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, there exists
Q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{n}\leqslant(1+{\rm o}(1))H_{s}
and sets
\tilde{A}_{n}\subseteq(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
and
\tilde{B}_{n}\subseteq\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}
such that

(I)

|(A-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash\tilde{A}_{n}|={\rm o}(Q_{n})
,

(II)

|B\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash\tilde{B}_{n}|={\rm o}(Q_{n})
, and

(III)

|(\tilde{A}_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}\tilde{B}_{n})\backslash((A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\})|={\rm o}(Q_{n})
.

Now, by
(II)
and the assumption
B\sim_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{N}\backslash h\mathbb{N})-b_{0}
, we have that
B^{\prime}_{n}=(\tilde{B}_{n}+b_{0})\backslash h\mathbb{N}
satisfies
|B^{\prime}_{n}|=\left(1-\frac{1}{h}-{\rm o}(1)\right)H_{s}
.
Let
A^{\prime}_{n}=(\tilde{A}_{n}+a_{n})\cap h\mathbb{N}
.
Then by our assumption on
A
, we have
|A^{\prime}_{n}|=(\alpha+{\rm o}(1))H_{s}
.
Therefore,

(\tilde{A}_{n}+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}\tilde{B}_{n})\cup(\tilde{A}_{n}+\{b_{1}-b_{0},b_{2}-b_{0}\})\\
\supseteq\underbrace{((A^{\prime}_{n}-a_{n})+_{\bmod{Q_{n}}}(B^{\prime}_{n}-b_{0}))}_{X}\cup\underbrace{((A^{\prime}_{n}-a_{n})+\{b_{1}-b_{0},b_{2}-b_{0}\})}_{Y},

where
X
is all but
{\rm o}(H_{s})
of
\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{n}-1\}\backslash(h\mathbb{N}-a_{n}-b_{0})
and
Y\subseteq h\mathbb{N}-a_{n}-b_{0}
has

|Y|=|A^{\prime}_{n}|+|(A^{\prime}_{n}+(b_{2}-b_{1}))\backslash A^{\prime}_{n}|=\left(\alpha+\frac{|(A^{\prime}_{n}+(b_{2}-b_{1}))\backslash A^{\prime}_{n}|}{H_{s}}+{\rm o}(1)\right)H_{s}.

Since
b_{1}-b_{0},b_{2}-b_{0}\in B
and
X\cap Y=\emptyset
, we have

\frac{|(A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}|}{H_{s}}\geqslant\frac{|X|+|Y|}{H_{s}}-{\rm o}(1)\\
=\left(\alpha+\beta+\frac{\left|\left((A+b_{2}-b_{1}-n)\backslash A\right)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\right|}{H_{s}}-{\rm o}(1)\right).

Averaging over
n\leqslant N_{s}
and taking a limit as
s\to\infty
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\alpha+\beta

\displaystyle=d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)=\lim_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{|(A+B-n)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}|}{H_{s}}

\displaystyle\geqslant\alpha+\beta+\limsup_{s\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{\left|\left((A+b_{2}-b_{1}-n)\backslash A\right)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\right|}{H_{s}}

\displaystyle=\alpha+\beta+\overline{d}_{\mathbf{N}}\left((A+b_{2}-b_{1})\backslash A\right).

Therefore,
A+b_{2}-b_{1}\sim_{\mathbf{N}}A
.
Hence,
P_{A}=\{n\in\mathbb{Z}:A+n\sim_{\mathbf{N}}A\}\supseteq B_{0}-B_{0}
.
But it is easily checked that
P_{A}
is a group, and the assumption that
B
meets every residue class in
\mathbb{N}
shows that
B_{0}-B_{0}
is not contained in any proper subgroup of
h\mathbb{Z}
, so
P_{A}\supseteq h\mathbb{Z}
.
That is,
A+h\sim_{\mathbf{N}}A
.

We will now show that for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
m\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
, there exists
b_{m}\in\{0,1,\ldots,h-1\}
such that
(B-m)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,h-1\}
differs from
(\{0,1,\ldots,h-1\}\backslash h\mathbb{N})-b_{m}
by
{\rm o}(H_{s})
many elements.
To this end, we apply
9.1
again with the roles of
A
and
B
reversed.
For each
s\in\mathbb{N}
, we may pick
n_{s}\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
with
n_{s}={\rm o}(N_{s})
such that

•

|(A-n_{s})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}|=(\alpha+{\rm o}(1))H_{s}
,

•

|(A-n_{s}+a_{n_{s}})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash h\mathbb{N}|={\rm o}(H_{s})
, and

•

\left|\left((A-n_{s}+a_{n_{s}}+h)\backslash(A-n_{s}+a_{n_{s}})\right)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\right|={\rm o}(H_{s})
.

Therefore, for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
m\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
,
9.1
provides
Q_{m}\in h\mathbb{N}
with
H_{s}\leqslant Q_{m}\leqslant(1+{\rm o}(1))H_{s}
and sets
\tilde{A}_{m}\subseteq(A-n_{s}+a_{n_{s}})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{m}-1\}
and
\tilde{B}_{m}\subseteq(B-m)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{m}-1\}
such that

(I)

|(A-n_{s}+a_{n_{s}})\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{m}-1\}\backslash\tilde{A}_{m}|={\rm o}(Q_{m})
,

(II)

|(B-m)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{m}-1\}\backslash\tilde{B}_{m}|={\rm o}(Q_{m})
,

(III)

|(\tilde{A}_{m}+_{\bmod{Q_{m}}}\tilde{B}_{m})\backslash((A+B-n_{s}+a_{n_{s}}-m)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{m}-1\})|={\rm o}(Q_{m})
,

(IV)

\tilde{A}_{m}\subseteq h\mathbb{Z}\cap\{0,1,\ldots,Q_{m}-1\}
, and

(V)

|(\tilde{A}_{m}+h)\backslash\tilde{A}_{m}|={\rm o}(Q_{m})
.

We may write

\tilde{A}_{m}+_{\bmod{Q_{m}}}\tilde{B}_{m}=\bigcup_{j=0}^{h-1}(\tilde{A}_{m}+_{\bmod{Q_{m}}}\tilde{B}_{m,j}),

where
\tilde{B}_{m,j}=\{x\in\tilde{B}_{m}:x\equiv j\pmod{h}\}
.
Since
\tilde{A}_{m}\subseteq h\mathbb{Z}
, this union is a disjoint one.
Letting
A^{\prime}_{m}=\frac{\tilde{A}_{m}}{h}
and
B^{\prime}_{m,j}=\frac{\tilde{B}_{m,j}-j}{h}
, we have

\left|\tilde{A}_{m}+_{\bmod{Q_{m}}}\tilde{B}_{m}\right|=\sum_{j=0}^{h-1}\left|A^{\prime}_{m}+_{\bmod{\frac{Q_{m}}{h}}}B^{\prime}_{m,j}\right|.

Now,
|(A^{\prime}_{m}+1)\backslash A^{\prime}_{m}|={\rm o}(Q_{m}/h)
, so
A^{\prime}_{m}
meets every residue class modulo at most
D_{s}
for some
D_{s}\to\infty
.
Hence, by Theorem
4.4
, if
B^{\prime}_{m,j}\neq\emptyset
, then

\left|A^{\prime}_{m}+_{\bmod{\frac{Q_{m}}{h}}}B^{\prime}_{m,j}\right|\geqslant\min\left\{|A^{\prime}_{m}|+|B^{\prime}_{m,j}|-\frac{Q_{m}}{hD_{s}},\frac{Q_{m}}{h}\right\}.

Now,
|A^{\prime}_{m}|=|\tilde{A}_{m}|=(\alpha-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))Q_{m}
, so if
|B^{\prime}_{m,j}|=\beta_{m,j}\frac{Q_{m}}{h}
, then

\frac{\left|A^{\prime}_{m}+_{\bmod{\frac{Q_{m}}{h}}}B^{\prime}_{m,j}\right|}{Q_{m}/h}\geqslant\min\{h\alpha+\beta_{m,j},1\}-{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1).

By Lemma
11.2
(with
q=1
), we conclude that if

|(A+B-n_{s}+a_{n_{s}}-m)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}|\leqslant(\alpha+\beta+{\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1))H_{s},

(11.1)

then
\min_{0\leqslant j\leqslant h-1}\beta_{m,j}={\rm o}_{s\to\infty}(1)
.
In this case, taking
b_{m}
such that
\beta_{m,b_{m}}={\rm o}(1)
, it follows that
B
has a local decomposition
(B-m)\cap\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}=(B_{m,0}\cup B_{m,1})-b_{m}
with
|B_{m,0}|={\rm o}(H_{s})
and
B_{m,1}\subseteq\{0,1,\ldots,H_{s}-1\}\backslash h\mathbb{Z}
with
|B_{m,1}|=\left(1-\frac{1}{h}-{\rm o}(1)\right)H_{s}
.
But (
11.1
) holds for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
m\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
(see the argument in Case 2 of the proof of
9.2
), so we have the desired local structure of
B
for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
m\in\{N_{s-1}+1,\ldots,N_{s}\}
.

To summarize our progress so far, we have shown that locally on intervals of length
H_{s}
,
A
is nearly contained in a single residue class mod
h
, and
B
looks like the union of
h-1
residue classes.
However, so far we have allowed for the possibility that the specific residue classes involved vary with the choice of interval of length
H_{s}
.
We now seek to rule out this possibility, first for
A
and then for
B
.

Suppose for contradiction that
A
contains two elements
a_{1},a_{2}\in A
with
a_{1}\not\equiv a_{2}\pmod{h}
.
Then from the local description of the set
B
, we have

d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)\geqslant d_{\mathbf{N}}(\{a_{1},a_{2}\}+B)=1,

which contradicts the assumption
d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)=\alpha+\beta<1
.
Thus,
A\subseteq h\mathbb{N}-a_{0}
for some
a_{0}\in\{0,\ldots,h-1\}
.

Recall that
B_{0}=(B+b_{0})\cap h\mathbb{N}
.
Let
B_{1}=(B+b_{0})\backslash h\mathbb{N}
so that
B=(B_{0}\cup B_{1})-b_{0}
.
First we claim that
\underline{d}_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B_{1})\geqslant 1-\frac{1}{h}=\beta
.
To see this, note that

\underline{d}_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B_{1})\geqslant\liminf_{s\to\infty}\frac{\left|(A\cap[N_{s}])+(B\cap[H_{s}])\right|}{N_{s}}.

Since
\mathbf{H}\succ\mathbf{H}_{G,A}
and
d(A)=\alpha>0
, we have that for all but
{\rm o}(N_{s})
many
n\in[N_{s}]
, there exists
t_{n}\in\mathbb{N}
with
t_{n}={\rm o}(H_{s})
such that
n+t_{n}\in A
.
Adding the elements
n+t_{n}
to
B\cap[H_{s}]
, we have

\liminf_{s\to\infty}\frac{\left|(A\cap[N_{s}])+(B\cap[H_{s}])\right|}{N_{s}}\geqslant\beta.

Since
A+B_{0}
is disjoint from
A+B_{1}
, we deduce that
\overline{d}_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B_{0})=d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B)-\underline{d}_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B_{1})\leqslant\alpha
.
But
A+B_{0}
contains a shifted copy of
A
, so
d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B_{0})=\alpha
.
We will use this observation to show
d_{\mathbf{N}}(B_{0})=0
, whence
B\sim_{\mathbf{N}}(\mathbb{N}\backslash h\mathbb{N})-b_{0}
.

Suppose for contradiction that
d_{\mathbf{N}}(B_{0})=\delta>0
.
Let
A^{\prime}=(A+a_{0})/h
and
B^{\prime}=B_{0}/h
.
Note that
A+B_{0}=h(A^{\prime}+B^{\prime})-a_{0}
, so
d_{\mathbf{N}/h}(A^{\prime}+B^{\prime})=h\cdot d_{\mathbf{N}}(A+B_{0})=h\alpha
.
Similarly,
d_{\mathbf{N}/h}(A^{\prime})=h\alpha
and
d_{\mathbf{N}/h}(B^{\prime})=h\delta
.
Then for each
s\in\mathbb{N}
, we may apply Lemma
4.3
to find
m_{s}\leqslant(1-\frac{h\delta}{2})N_{s}/h
such that
B^{\prime}
has Schnirelmann density at least
\frac{h\delta}{2}+{\rm o}(1)
on the interval
\{m_{s}+1,\ldots,\lfloor N_{s}/h\rfloor\}
.
By Lemma
11.3
, there exists
n_{s}={\rm o}(N_{s})
such that
A^{\prime}
has Schnirelmann density
h\alpha+{\rm o}(1)
on
\{n_{s}+1,\ldots,n_{s}+N_{s}/h-m_{s}\}
.
Moreover, since
A^{\prime}+1\sim_{\mathbf{N}/h}A^{\prime}
, we may pick
n_{s}
to itself be an element of
A^{\prime}
.
Observe that for a fixed
b^{\prime}\in B^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle(A^{\prime}+B^{\prime})

\displaystyle\cap[N_{s}/h]

\displaystyle\supseteq

\displaystyle\penalty\ \underbrace{\left(b^{\prime}+(A^{\prime}\cap[N_{s}/h-b^{\prime}])\right)}_{\sim A^{\prime}\cap[N_{s}/h]}

\displaystyle\cup\underbrace{\left(B^{\prime}\cap\{m_{s}+1,\ldots,N_{s}/h\}+A^{\prime}\cap\{n_{s},n_{s}+1,\ldots,n_{s}+\lfloor N_{s}/h\rfloor-m_{s}\}\right)\cap[N_{s}/h]}_{\text{density}\penalty\ \geqslant h\alpha+\frac{h\delta}{2}(1-h\alpha)-{\rm o}(1)\penalty\ \text{in}\penalty\ \{n_{s}+m_{s}+1,\ldots,n_{s}+\lfloor N_{s}/h\rfloor\}\penalty\ \text{by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{cor: Schnirelmann up to N}}},

so
d_{\mathbf{N}/h}(A^{\prime}+B^{\prime})\geqslant h\alpha+\frac{h^{2}\delta^{2}}{4}(1-h\alpha)
.
But
\alpha+\beta<1
, so
h\alpha<1
and we obtain a strict inequality
d_{\mathbf{N}/h}(A^{\prime}+B^{\prime})>h\alpha
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Corollary 11.4
.

Let
A,B\subseteq\mathbb{N}
with
d(A)=\alpha>0
and
d(B)=\beta>0
such that
\alpha+\beta<1
.
Suppose
B
meets every residue class in
\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\mathbf{N}=(N_{s})_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
be an increasing sequence with
